# 07. GenAIOps basics
> **Track:** OpenShift AI Fundamentals — Corporate/L3
> **Audience:** Senior OpenShift administrators, platform engineers, MLOps engineers, SREs, security engineers, architects, and technical leads
> **Version basis:** Aligned to the Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5 documentation stream available in July 2026. Always confirm support matrices, API stability, operator channels, and component status for your exact subscription and cluster release.
> **Hands-on project:** Build and operationalize a governed retrieval-augmented generation service
---
## Executive definition
GenAIOps extends MLOps for foundation models, prompts, retrieval systems, vector data, evaluation, safety, cost, latency, agents, and rapidly changing model dependencies.
This module treats the subject as an enterprise platform capability rather than a product-demo feature. The emphasis is architecture, ownership, controls, operability, failure handling, repeatability, and measurable production outcomes.
## Learning outcomes
- Design a GenAI delivery lifecycle.
- Measure quality and safety.
- Control inference cost.
- Operate RAG and agent workloads.
- Translate the topic into architecture diagrams, operational procedures, security controls, and acceptance tests.
- Explain trade-offs to application owners, data scientists, auditors, and senior management.
- Diagnose failures using a layer-by-layer and evidence-driven troubleshooting method.
## Scope and assumptions
- A supported Red Hat OpenShift cluster is available or planned.
- The OpenShift AI subscription, operator channel, and support matrix are validated before implementation.
- Identity, DNS, certificates, storage classes, ingress, registry access, and monitoring are treated as shared platform dependencies.
- GPU or alternative accelerator capacity is optional for fundamentals but mandatory for many production training and large-model inference workloads.
- S3-compatible object storage is planned for datasets, pipeline artifacts, model artifacts, and other lifecycle data when required.
- Production changes are delivered through Git, peer review, automated validation, and controlled promotion.
- Examples use placeholders and must not be copied into production without security review.
- Feature status can differ between General Availability, Technology Preview, Developer Preview, and community upstream implementations.
## Topic focus map
### Focus 1: Foundation Models
- Define what **foundation models** means for GenAIOps basics.
- Identify the owner, consumer, supporting team, approval authority, and escalation path for foundation models.
- Document the normal flow, failure flow, recovery flow, evidence requirements, and version constraints for foundation models.
- Create measurable acceptance criteria for foundation models, including functionality, security, reliability, performance, and operability.
- Decide what is standardized centrally and what remains project-specific for foundation models.
- Review how foundation models changes between development, test, production, edge, hybrid-cloud, and disconnected environments.

## Component relationship map
### Component 1: model catalog
- **Purpose:** Describe why model catalog is relevant to GenAIOps basics and which user or controller consumes it.
- **Inputs:** Identify configuration, credentials, data, artifacts, quotas, network access, and dependencies required by model catalog.
- **Outputs:** Record APIs, artifacts, logs, metrics, status conditions, and user-visible outcomes produced by model catalog.
- **Dependencies:** Map OpenShift APIs, storage, DNS, certificates, identity, Operators, and external services used by model catalog.
- **Security:** Define service accounts, RBAC, secrets, network policy, image trust, audit, and data classification for model catalog.
- **Reliability:** Define health checks, replica strategy, disruption controls, backup scope, recovery objective, and alerting for model catalog.
- **Operations:** Establish installation, configuration, upgrade, troubleshooting, support, and decommission procedures for model catalog.

## Enterprise architecture layers
### Layer 1: Business Outcome And Product Management
1. State the responsibility of the business outcome and product management layer for GenAIOps basics.
2. Identify authoritative configuration sources for the business outcome and product management layer.
3. List upstream and downstream dependencies of the business outcome and product management layer.
4. Define isolation boundaries and data classification at the business outcome and product management layer.
5. Define SLIs for availability, latency, throughput, quality, saturation, and errors where applicable.
6. Define support ownership and escalation from project team to platform team to vendor.
7. Capture evidence through logs, events, metrics, traces, manifests, approvals, and test results.
8. Design rollback and disaster-recovery behavior for failures at the business outcome and product management layer.

## Reference manifests and command patterns

> These examples are instructional baselines. Validate API versions and component support on your exact OpenShift AI release.

### Project namespace

```yaml
apiVersion: project.openshift.io/v1
kind: Project
metadata:
  name: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/part-of: openshift-ai-fundamentals
    training.redhat.com/topic: "genaiops-basics"
    environment: lab
    owner: ai-platform-team
```

### ResourceQuota baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: ai-team-quota
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  hard:
    requests.cpu: "8"
    requests.memory: 32Gi
    limits.cpu: "16"
    limits.memory: 64Gi
    requests.storage: 500Gi
    persistentvolumeclaims: "10"
    count/pods: "50"
```

### LimitRange baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: ai-team-defaults
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  limits:
    - type: Container
      defaultRequest:
        cpu: "250m"
        memory: 512Mi
      default:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
```

### Default-deny NetworkPolicy

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: default-deny
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    - Egress
```

### ServiceAccount for controlled automation

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: ai-pipeline-runner
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
automountServiceAccountToken: true
```

### Role for project-level read operations

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: ai-project-reader
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
rules:
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "statefulsets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
```

### RoleBinding

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: ai-project-readers
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
subjects:
  - kind: Group
    name: ai-data-scientists
    apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: ai-project-reader
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

### S3-compatible data connection secret pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: lab-object-storage
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
    opendatahub.io/managed: "true"
  annotations:
    openshift.io/display-name: Lab object storage
type: Opaque
stringData:
  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_S3_ENDPOINT: https://s3.example.internal
  AWS_S3_BUCKET: ai-lab
  AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-1
```

### GitOps application pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: genaiops-basics
  namespace: openshift-gitops
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://git.example.internal/ai-platform/fundamentals.git
    targetRevision: main
    path: topics/genaiops-basics
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
```

### Baseline validation commands

```bash
oc whoami
oc version
oc get clusterversion
oc get csv -A | grep -i -E 'rhods|opendatahub|nvidia|kueue|serverless|servicemesh'
oc get datasciencecluster -A -o wide
oc get dscinitialization -A -o wide
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-operator
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get routes -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100
```
## From-scratch corporate project
### Project goal: Build and operationalize a governed retrieval-augmented generation service
### Phase 1: Business framing and success criteria
**Objective:** Apply business framing and success criteria specifically to **GenAIOps basics**.
1. Define the deliverable and an accountable owner for model catalog, with explicit consideration of foundation models.
2. Record assumptions and unresolved questions for vLLM, with explicit consideration of prompt lifecycle.
3. Create objective entry and exit criteria for RAG stack, with explicit consideration of RAG.
4. Identify dependencies and support boundaries for OGX, with explicit consideration of vector stores.
5. Capture security and compliance requirements for embedding model, with explicit consideration of evaluation.
6. Define evidence retained for audit for vector database, with explicit consideration of guardrails.
7. Write an automation or gitops approach for document ingestion, with explicit consideration of inference optimization.
8. Define validation commands and expected results for prompt templates, with explicit consideration of token economics.
9. Document failure modes and rollback for evaluation harness, with explicit consideration of agent operations.
10. Review capacity, cost, and licensing impact for model serving, with explicit consideration of responsible AI.
11. Peer-review the design with platform and application teams for observability, with explicit consideration of foundation models.
12. Obtain change approval before production for policy controls, with explicit consideration of prompt lifecycle.
**Phase evidence:**
- Approved record for business framing and success criteria.
- Configuration or design artifact stored in Git for genaiops-basics.
- Test output showing both successful and intentionally failed paths.
- Named owner and escalation path for unresolved risk.

## Detailed hands-on lab sequence
### Lab 1: Collect cluster and operator inventory
**Goal:** Collect cluster and operator inventory for GenAIOps basics, using model catalog as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate foundation models, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate prompt lifecycle, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate RAG, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate vector stores, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate evaluation, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate guardrails, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate inference optimization, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate token economics, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The model catalog state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to prompt lifecycle.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.
### Lab 2: Validate identity and group mappings
**Goal:** Validate identity and group mappings for GenAIOps basics, using vLLM as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for validate identity and group mappings; validate prompt lifecycle, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for validate identity and group mappings; validate RAG, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for validate identity and group mappings; validate vector stores, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for validate identity and group mappings; validate evaluation, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for validate identity and group mappings; validate guardrails, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for validate identity and group mappings; validate inference optimization, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for validate identity and group mappings; validate token economics, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for validate identity and group mappings; validate agent operations, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The vLLM state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to RAG.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.
### Lab 3: Create a governed project namespace
**Goal:** Create a governed project namespace for GenAIOps basics, using RAG stack as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**

## Enterprise control catalogue
### Control 1: Identity And Access
- **Policy statement:** GenAIOps basics must implement documented identity and access controls for model catalog.
- **Design requirement:** The control must account for RAG, tenant boundaries, and version-specific support constraints.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, manifest, configuration export, approval record, and automated test result.
- **Operational evidence:** Metrics, logs, alerts, access review, capacity report, incident record, or restore test as applicable.
- **Failure criterion:** The control fails when unauthorized, untraceable, unsupported, unmonitored, or unrecoverable behavior is possible.
- **Review frequency:** Review after material platform changes and on the organization's scheduled control cycle.

## SRE and operational readiness
### Reliability objective 1: Dashboard Availability
- **Indicator:** Define how dashboard availability is measured for GenAIOps basics.
- **Objective:** Select a target from business criticality, error budget, dependency reliability, and support capability.
- **Telemetry:** Identify source metrics, log queries, events, synthetic tests, and dashboard panels.
- **Alert:** Alert only when user impact or error-budget risk requires action.
- **Runbook:** Include diagnosis, mitigation, escalation, rollback, and post-recovery validation.
- **Review:** Evaluate trend, saturation, false positives, and improvement actions during service review.

## Troubleshooting playbook
### Scenario 1: Dashboard Is Unavailable
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating GenAIOps basics and is first observed around embedding model.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Taint, toleration, affinity, or node-selector mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get pods -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass prompt lifecycle controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 2: User Cannot See A Project
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating GenAIOps basics and is first observed around model serving.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Artifact metadata mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get csv -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass RAG controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.

- **Second command:** `oc get storageclass`

## Production design decisions
### Decision 1: Dedicated Versus Shared Openshift Cluster
- **Question:** Which dedicated versus shared OpenShift cluster pattern best supports GenAIOps basics?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

## Corporate scenario drills
### Drill 1: Regulated Financial Service

## Expanded glossary
### Term 1: AI platform
- **Definition:** A managed set of services, controls, and workflows for creating and operating AI solutions.
- **Relevance:** In GenAIOps basics, relate AI platform to model catalog, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 2: MLOps
- **Definition:** Practices that automate and govern the machine-learning lifecycle.
- **Relevance:** In GenAIOps basics, relate MLOps to vLLM, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 3: GenAIOps
- **Definition:** Operational practices for foundation-model, RAG, prompt, agent, evaluation, and inference lifecycles.
- **Relevance:** In GenAIOps basics, relate GenAIOps to RAG stack, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 4: ModelOps
- **Definition:** Portfolio-wide governance and operations for models, versions, approvals, deployments, and risk.
- **Relevance:** In GenAIOps basics, relate ModelOps to OGX, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 5: Workbench
- **Definition:** A project-scoped interactive development environment such as JupyterLab or code-server.
- **Relevance:** In GenAIOps basics, relate Workbench to embedding model, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 6: Pipeline
- **Definition:** A repeatable workflow of containerized tasks for data and model lifecycle automation.
- **Relevance:** In GenAIOps basics, relate Pipeline to vector database, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 7: Model registry
- **Definition:** A governed metadata and artifact catalog for model versions and lifecycle state.
- **Relevance:** In GenAIOps basics, relate Model registry to document ingestion, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 8: Model catalog
- **Definition:** A curated discovery source for models that can be evaluated, registered, or deployed.
- **Relevance:** In GenAIOps basics, relate Model catalog to prompt templates, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.

## Final production-readiness statement
A production implementation of **GenAIOps basics** is ready only when functionality, security, reliability, performance, supportability, cost, auditability, and recovery have objective evidence. A successful demonstration is not equivalent to an operationally supported enterprise service.
